```json
        "content": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\nWe test the three predictions of the confirmation bias hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Table~\\ref{tab:independence} (Appendix) reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$. Across four Mistral-7B seeds, $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$ yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp, indicating seed-level constraint quality matters beyond independence. Positive $\\Delta$ requires both sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ degrades). All four model families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\\times$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints, confirming degeneration (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}).\n\n\\looseness=-1 Bias generalizes across scale: $4.97\\times$ [3.98, 6.37] (Mistral, 12-trace), $3.93\\times$ [2.44, 7.11] (Qwen-14B, 12-trace), $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA, 4-trace$^{\\dag}$), all $> 1.0$ (95\\% bootstrap CIs for Mistral/Qwen; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}; weighted BRs in Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-bias}). SCB persists across nine models from 7B to frontier (BR $1.66$--$4.97\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}). Constraints use trace-specific variables (56.4\\% zero cross-answer overlap), collapsing aggregation to majority voting.\n\n\\looseness=-1 Two controls rule out answer leakage. First, BR is uniform across early ($5.37$), mid ($5.25$), and late ($5.16$) reasoning steps (Fisher $p = 0.71$); high-consensus errors (SC $\\geq 10/12$) amplify to $\\text{BR} = 18.8\\times$ versus $2.7\\times$ for low-consensus ($p < 0.001$). Second, removing the answer sentence from CoT traces before extraction yields no BR decrease across three truncation strategies (Wilcoxon $p \\geq 0.12$, smallest $p = 0.12$, conservative truncation; per-problem mean BR: full $2.79$, truncated $2.26$--$2.66$, computed over problems with finite ratio) despite significant accuracy loss (McNemar $p < 0.013$). SCB is consistent with a distributional prior embedded throughout reasoning, not answer contamination.\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\nWe define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:process-sim}\n\\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \\frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \\sum_{i < j} \\cos(\\mathbf{v}_i^q, \\mathbf{v}_j^q),\n\\end{equation}\n\\looseness=-1 where $\\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, mean ProcessSim $= 0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.46$: traces share reasoning processes even when answers diverge. The gap generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}), ranging from $0.31$ (LLaMA) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), revealing hidden process homogeneity that answer diversity masks. Under cross-model extraction, SICA gain decreases monotonically with ProcessSim ($\\rho = -1.0$, $p = 0.008$; Table~\\ref{tab:process-gradient}), modulated by self-bias severity.\n\n\n\\subsection{Cumulative Information Lift}\n\\label{sec:cil}\n\n\\looseness=-1 We unify the preceding diagnostics under \\textit{Cumulative Information Lift} (CIL):\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:cil-main}\n\\text{CIL}(V, G) = \\log_2 \\frac{P(V \\text{ correct} \\mid G \\text{ wrong})}{P(V \\text{ correct} \\mid G \\text{ correct})}.\n\\end{equation}\n$\\text{CIL} > 0$ when verifier $V$ preferentially corrects generator $G$'s errors; $\\text{CIL} < 0$ indicates shared bias. Table~\\ref{tab:cil_main} reports CIL for eight verifier--generator pairs. All cross-architecture NLI pairs on ProofWriter yield $\\text{CIL} > 0$, while same-model SICA yields $\\text{CIL} < 0$. CIL strongly predicts combo gain (Spearman $\\rho = 0.881$, $p = 0.004$; Pearson $r = 0.854$, $p = 0.008$), unifying three findings: (1)~SCB corresponds to $\\text{CIL}(\\text{self}, G) \\ll 0$; (2)~cross-architecture escape succeeds when $\\text{CIL} > 0$; (3)~CIL decays toward zero as generator capability rises, explaining the aggregation ceiling.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\caption{Cumulative Information Lift across eight verifier--generator conditions. $\\text{CIL} > 0$: cross-arch NLI on ProofWriter. $\\text{CIL} < 0$: same-model SICA or weak capability gap.}\n\\label{tab:cil_main}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator--Verifier & Dataset & CIL & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral $\\times$ BART-NLI & PW & $+$1.86 & $+$5.17 \\\\\nMistral $\\times$ DeBERTa-NLI & PW & $+$1.67 & $+$6.67 \\\\\nMistral $\\times$ RoBERTa-NLI & PW & $+$1.42 & $+$4.17 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen-14B $\\times$ DeBERTa-NLI & PW & $-$0.21 & $+$0.33 \\\\\nMistral $\\times$ DeBERTa-NLI & FOLIO & $-$0.47 & $+$2.94 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral $\\times$ SICA & FOLIO & $-$2.93 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nQwen-14B $\\times$ SICA & PW & $-$4.21 & $-$2.50 \\\\\nMistral $\\times$ SICA & PW & $-$4.61 & $-$2.83 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\nFurther diagnostics confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}). One positive signal emerges: Z3-based semantic deduplication removes ${\\sim}$10\\% additional constraints beyond syntactic dedup and yields a small but significant gain ($p = 0.013$), confirming that the formal channel adds value when redundancy is reduced. The bottleneck is not Z3 but the quality of input constraints: confirmation bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$) overwhelms the deduplication gain.\n\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning four categories---sampling diversity (temperature, multi-T, diverse prompting), extraction quality (few-shot, iterative refinement, confidence filtering), aggregation (constraint weighting, semantic dedup, MAX-SAT variants), and multi-agent (debate, self-refine, critique)---all produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Tables~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}, \\ref{tab:remediation}, \\ref{tab:additional_interventions}). Of these, 12 represent principled strategies targeting distinct failure modes (independent constraint generation, contrastive extraction, debate-augmented reasoning, etc.), while 15 are parameter variations (temperature, prompt template, few-shot count). At the method level, Debate achieves the lowest $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$); all other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, confirming a same-model ceiling (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}). We note an important gap: we do not evaluate trained constraint extractors (e.g., fine-tuned on oracle annotations). Our oracle controls show the extraction-quality ceiling is $+29.90$\\,pp---we consider trained extractors the most promising future direction.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\textwidth]{figures/fig_app_interventions_summary.pdf}\n\\caption{Summary of 27 intervention strategies grouped by four categories. All produce neutral-to-negative accuracy changes relative to Self-Consistency. The grey band indicates $\\pm 1$\\,pp noise zone.}\n\\label{fig:interventions-summary}\n\\end{figure*}",

        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 83
      }
    },
```

```json
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/discussion.tex",
        "content": "\\vspace{-1mm}\n\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Self-verification and BR magnitude.}\n\\looseness=-1 Our bias ratio ($1.66$--$4.97\\times$ across nine models from 7B to frontier; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}) quantifies why self-correction fails without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification, chen2025sets}: extraction and generation share distributional biases, with nearly 5:1 on incorrect problems. Cross-model extraction gain requires both diverse traces (low $\\kappa_{\\text{proc}}$) and substantial self-bias (high BR); neither alone suffices (Table~\\ref{tab:process-gradient}). Any self-verification pipeline deriving criteria from the same model faces this bottleneck.\n\n\\paragraph{Test-time scaling and practitioner implications.}\n\\looseness=-1 $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$ means sampling beyond a dozen traces yields diminishing independent information; the pattern intensifies with capability (Qwen3 thinking: $\\kappa = 0.91$, $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.09$), creating a scaling paradox for o1/R1-style models \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}. All non-destructive interventions cluster at $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ \\citep{krogh1994neural}; Debate reaches $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\\,pp). Cross-architecture escape succeeds ($p = 0.003$) because encoder-only and decoder-only models have structurally different error profiles. Two diagnostics predict failure: $C_0 < 5\\%$ means SICA reduces to SC; SC $\\leq 40\\%$ risks error amplification ($-2.83$\\,pp). For intermediate-SC models, gain tracks the capability gap monotonically ($p = 0.003$); the bottleneck is extraction quality, as oracle constraints succeed ($+29.90$\\,pp, $+14.22$\\,pp).\n",
        "numLines": 10,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 10
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/discussion.tex"
  },
```

```json
        "content": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\n\\label{sec:exp-setup}\n\nWe evaluate SICA against Self-Consistency (SC) across six models, three reasoning domains, and over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances), with diagnostic analyses of confirmation bias and process-level independence (\\S\\ref{sec:analysis}).\n\n\\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B). Frontier extension (gpt-4o, gpt-4.1, gpt-5.5, o3, Gemini-2.5-Pro) evaluates SCB universality (Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}).\n\n\\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, with gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$). StrategyQA~\\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$) serves as a domain-formalizability diagnostic. GSM8K~\\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\\%$).\n\n\\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$, extraction at $T_{\\text{ext}}{=}0.3$, $\\alpha{=}0.5$, Z3 timeout 10s. McNemar exact test ($\\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\\% bootstrap CIs ($10{,}000$ resamples) for $n \\geq 200$. SICA incurs $4.5{\\times}$ SC wall-clock; extraction accounts for 76\\% of overhead.\n\n\n\\subsection{Main Results}\n\\label{sec:main-results}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all conditions sorted by SC baseline, revealing three observation-driven accuracy regimes.\n\n\\begin{table*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{SICA vs.\\ SC across all conditions (sorted by SC). No non-degenerate condition shows a significant gain after BH correction ($m{=}16$). $\\Delta$ = SICA $-$ SC (pp). Symbols: $^b$gold ICL; $\\dagger$\\,$n < 100$; \\textbf{bold}: $p < 0.05$; {*}\\,uncorrected $p < 0.05$ (BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$); {***}\\,oracle $p < 0.001$; $^{4s}$\\,4-seed confirmatory (unmarked: single-seed exploratory).}\n\\label{tab:main_results}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrl@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Domain & $n$ & SC (\\%) & SICA (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type C: error amplification (SC $\\leq$ 40\\%)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 600 & 39.33 & 36.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}$ & 0.014{*} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Intermediate SC (SC $\\sim$50--55\\%, Mistral-7B only)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 200 & 51.00 & 51.50 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s123) & 204 & 54.41 & 57.35 & $+$2.94 & 0.070 \\\\\nMistral-7B$^{4s}$ & FOLIO & 204 & 55.88 & 59.31 & $+$3.43 & 0.092 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s456) & 204 & 55.88 & 55.88 & 0.00 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s789) & 204 & 54.41 & 53.92 & $-$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type A: confirmation bias (SC $\\geq$ 60\\%)}} \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & FOLIO & 204 & 66.18 & 61.76 & $-$4.41 & 0.093 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & StrategyQA & 200 & 72.00 & 74.00 & $+$2.00 & 0.424 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO (ICL)$^b$ & 199 & 74.37 & 74.87 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 74.00 & 71.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.50}$ & 0.011{*} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.49 & 74.51 & $-$0.98 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.98 & 76.47 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 200 & 78.50 & 78.00 & $-$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 84.80 & 84.31 & $-$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 85.00 & 85.67 & $+$0.67 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B (think) & FOLIO & 204 & 86.27 & 86.76 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Degenerate ($C_0 < 5\\%$)}} \\\\\nQwQ-32B & AIME 2024$^\\dagger$ & 22 & 72.70 & 72.70 & 0.00 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & GSM8K$^\\dagger$ & 30 & 96.67 & 96.67 & 0.00 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Oracle upper bound (gold logical annotations + Z3)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 55.88 & \\textbf{85.78} & $\\mathbf{+29.90}$ & $1.0{\\times}10^{-13}${***} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.49 & \\textbf{89.71} & $\\mathbf{+14.22}$ & $2.0{\\times}10^{-7}${***} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & ProofWriter & 600 & 74.00 & \\textbf{91.17} & $\\mathbf{+17.17}$ & $9.21{\\times}10^{-15}${***} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table*}\n\n\\looseness=-1 No non-degenerate condition shows a significant SICA effect after FDR control (BH, $m{=}16$). Two show nominally significant degradation: Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); adjusted $p = 0.112$ for both. The intermediate-SC region (50--55\\%) contains the only positive trends, none reaching $p < 0.05$. Four-seed replication confirms seed-level noise: two of four seeds produce null or negative $\\Delta$, and the aggregate ($+3.43$\\,pp) does not reach significance ($p = 0.092$). TOST equivalence tests \\citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp confirm 4 equivalent, 2 genuinely different (both negative: Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter), 10 underpowered---reflecting the power limitations at $n{=}200$--$204$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:tost}). The $\\pm 2$\\,pp margin corresponds to the lower bound of gains reported in the SC literature \\citep{wang2023sc} (2--5\\,pp); $\\pm 1$\\,pp yields 0 equivalent conditions (0/2/14), $\\pm 3$\\,pp yields 10 (10/0/6). This absolute margin is more conservative at high baselines ($\\pm 2.4\\%$ relative at 85\\%) than low ones ($\\pm 5.1\\%$ at 39\\%), working against our equivalence claim where SICA most plausibly helps (relative $\\pm 5\\%$ margin yields 8 equivalent; Appendix~\\ref{app:relative_tost}). We trace the failure below.\n\n\n\\subsection{Oracle Control}\n\\label{sec:oracle}\n\nReplacing self-extracted constraints with gold annotations (FOLIO logical formulae, ProofWriter DSL), Oracle-SICA yields $+29.90$\\,pp on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($p < 10^{-13}$), $+14.22$\\,pp on Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO ($p = 2.0 \\times 10^{-7}$), and $+17.17$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 10^{-14}$). Z3 agrees with gold labels on 95.1\\% (FOLIO) and 97.1\\% (ProofWriter). The MAX-SAT mechanism is sound; Mistral-7B's best self-extracted $\\Delta$ ($+3.43$\\,pp) captures only 11.5\\% of its oracle ceiling. For Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter, the gap is wider still: self-extraction degrades accuracy ($-2.50$\\,pp) against an oracle ceiling of $+17.17$\\,pp, indicating that stronger models do not self-extract more effectively.\n\n\n",
        "numLines": 73,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 73
      }
```

```json
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nTest-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \\citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.\nThe dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.\nSC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.\n\\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.\nThe gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.\n\nConfidence-weighted \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \\citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.\nRecent neuro-symbolic methods \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.\nA simpler question precedes these: can the \\emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?\nNo prior work has systematically evaluated this.\n\nWe ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \\citep{austensmith1996}.\nWhen independence fails \\citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.\nMeasuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}}$ that reveals near-deterministic voting dynamics ($K_{\\text{eff}} \\approx 1.2$--$3.6$ for nominal $K = 12$; should be interpreted as an ordinal diagnostic rather than a literal effective sample size, \\S\\ref{sec:condorcet}): traces beyond a dozen add negligible independent information (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\nThe bottleneck runs deeper than answer-level agreement suggests.\nChain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.46$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\nWhen the same model extracts logical constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: on problems where the model errs, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\nSelf-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.\n\nWe study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nAcross six open-source models (7B--32B) plus five frontier models, three domains (FOLIO \\citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \\citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations with four-seed replication, twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all produce neutral or negative results (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}).\nPrior work shows LLMs struggle with natural-language self-correction \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect} and that LLM-generated logical formulas can be unreliable \\citep{stechly2025selfverification}. Symbolic consensus introduces an additional assumption: that formalizing reasoning into first-order logic checked by a provably sound solver (Z3) provides \\emph{independent} verification evidence. Unlike natural-language self-correction, where generation and evaluation share the same neural channel, our formal channel is provably sound---the failure is precisely localizable to the extraction step. The assumption fails not because Z3 is unreliable (99\\% of constraints are syntactically valid; only 3.4\\% are rejected, \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}), but because extraction inherits the model's biases, creating \\emph{formalized echo chambers}.\n\nOracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\\,pp Mistral-7B, $+14.22$\\,pp Qwen-14B on FOLIO, $p < 0.001$; \\S\\ref{sec:oracle}), yet self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of this ceiling.\nThe central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\\kappa$ to 0.15 but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\\,pp); other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, a same-model ceiling across three families (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\nA cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the confirmation bias: DeBERTa NLI yields $+6.67$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC---even under formal Z3-checked verification (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Rather than discovering a new diversity--accuracy tradeoff \\citep{krogh1994neural}, we quantify its specific form in LLM self-extraction via three diagnostics ($K_\\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$). Same-model $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across four methods and three families; twenty-seven strategies fail. Cross-architecture NLI yields $+6.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$; $w{=}3$), with a consistent monotonic capability-gap trend ($p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; \\S\\ref{sec:remediation}, \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Process-Level Independence.} CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$) across four models from three families; under cross-model extraction on frontier models, SICA gain decreases monotonically with process similarity ($\\rho = -1.0$, $p = 0.008$; \\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC).} A Pareto-efficient adaptive routing method that selectively invokes cross-architecture verification based on per-question trace agreement, recovering 83--90\\% of full-combo gain at 35--39\\% cost across two datasets (\\S\\ref{sec:drc}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n    node distance=0.3cm and 0.1cm,\n    box/.style={draw, rounded corners=2pt, minimum height=0.55cm,\n                text width=1.2cm, align=center, font=\\scriptsize},\n    arr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick},\n    darr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick, densely dashed},\n    lbl/.style={font=\\scriptsize, text=gray!70!black},\n    ibottle/.style={draw=red!70!black, densely dashed, rounded corners=3pt,\n                    inner sep=3pt},\n]\n\\node[box] (q) {Question $q$};\n\\node[box, right=of q] (sample) {Sample $K$\\\\traces};\n\\node[box, right=of sample] (extract) {Extract logical\\\\constraints};\n\\node[box, right=of extract] (dedup) {Dedup.\\ +\\\\weight};\n\\node[box, right=of dedup] (maxsat) {MAX-SAT\\\\(Z3)};\n\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of maxsat, fill=gray!12] (ans) {Answer $a^*$};\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of sample, fill=gray!12] (sc) {Majority\\\\vote};\n\n\\draw[arr] (q) -- (sample);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- (extract);\n\\draw[arr] (extract) -- (dedup);\n\\draw[arr] (dedup) -- (maxsat);\n\\draw[arr] (maxsat) -- (ans);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- node[lbl, right, yshift=0pt] {SC} (sc);\n\n\\node[lbl, above=0.01cm of maxsat] {SICA};\n\n\\node[ibottle, fit=(sample)(extract), label={[font=\\scriptsize\\itshape, text=red!70!black]above:Independence Bottleneck}] {};\n\n\\draw[darr, gray!60] ([xshift=-0.1cm]dedup.south) -- ++(0,-0.35) node[lbl, below, yshift=-0.05cm] {oracle: gold FOL};\n\\end{tikzpicture}}\n\\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts logical constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure}\n",
```

```text
11	\label{app:prompts}
12	
13	SICA uses domain-specific prompt templates for constraint extraction: one for mathematical reasoning (GSM8K, AIME) and one for logical reasoning (ProofWriter, FOLIO). Both share the same system prompt. The LLM is called with temperature $T{=}0.3$, max tokens $1024$, and structured JSON output mode. Up to 6 traces are processed in parallel via thread pooling, with 2 retries on failure (exponential backoff, base 0.5s).
14	
15	\paragraph{System Prompt.}
16	
17	\begin{lstlisting}
18	You are a constraint extraction tool. Output ONLY
19	valid JSON. Do NOT solve problems, reason about
20	correctness, or think step-by-step. Extract
21	constraints from the given trace exactly as stated.
22	\end{lstlisting}
23	
24	\paragraph{Mathematical Constraint Extraction (EXTRACTION\_PROMPT).}
25	
26	Used for GSM8K and AIME datasets. The placeholder \texttt{\{trace\}} is replaced with the reasoning trace at runtime.
27	
28	\begin{lstlisting}
29	Extract mathematical constraints from this reasoning
30	trace as a JSON object.
31	
32	Rules:
33	- type: "equation", "inequality", or "predicate"
34	- expression: human-readable, e.g. "x + y == 14"
35	- z3_formula: a single comparison expression in
36	  Python Z3 syntax
37	- source_step: step number where the constraint
38	  appears
39	- Use variable names from the trace
40	
41	z3_formula MUST follow these rules:
42	- Allowed operators: ==, !=, <, >, <=, >=, +, -, *,
43	  /, **, %
44	- Use individual named variables: a1, a2, a3...
45	  NOT arrays a[1] or sets A
46	- Expand all sums explicitly:
47	  "a1 + a2 + a3 == 55" NOT "sum([a1,a2,a3])"
48	- NO Python builtins: sum(), len(), max(), min(),
49	  list(), set(), range(), int()
```

```text
718	
719	Table~\ref{tab:cross_model_kappa} compares trace independence across three model families on the same benchmarks. On FOLIO ($n = 204$), all four models produce $\kappa \geq 0.41$: LLaMA-3.1-8B ($\kappa = 0.41$), Mistral-7B ($\kappa = 0.44$), Qwen2.5-7B ($\kappa = 0.66$), and Qwen2.5-14B ($\kappa = 0.74$). The bottleneck is architecture-independent---no family escapes the correlation floor. Within the Qwen family, $\kappa$ increases from 0.66 (7B) to 0.74 (14B), but Mistral-7B (7B) and LLaMA-3.1-8B (8B) differ by $\Delta\kappa = 0.03$ despite belonging to different families, suggesting that cross-family variation ($\Delta\kappa$ up to 0.22 at similar scale) exceeds within-family scale effects ($\Delta\kappa = 0.08$). Disentangling scale from architecture requires controlled same-family experiments across $\geq$3 scales.
```

```text
649	\section{Independence-Accuracy Scatter Plot}
650	\label{app:kappa-scatter}
651	
652	\begin{figure}[h]
653	\centering
654	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/f6_kappa_accuracy_scatter}
655	\caption{Independence-accuracy trade-off across aggregation methods (Mistral-7B, filled markers) and model families (LLaMA-3.1-8B, Qwen2.5-7B, Qwen2.5-14B; open markers) on FOLIO ($n = 204$). Same-model methods cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$ (gray band), while cross-model SC shows stronger correlations ($\kappa = 0.41$--$0.74$) at higher accuracy. Debate breaks correlation ($\kappa = 0.15$) at catastrophic accuracy cost. Dashed line: Mistral-7B SC baseline.}
656	\label{fig:kappa-accuracy}
657	\end{figure}
658	
659	\begin{table}[h]
660	\centering
661	\small
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01MwrYNbq4JTZxUvjJDYxfum`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}
2	\label{sec:solutions}
3	\label{sec:cross-arch}
4	
5	\paragraph{Method.}
6	We test with DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), whose (premise, conclusion) structure suits NLI classification.
7	
8	\paragraph{Main result.}
9	\looseness=-1 DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\%) and 55.00\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+0.49$\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+6.67$\,pp ($p < 0.001$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+11.00$\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\ $p = 0.112$; Table~\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ (adj.\ $p = 6.78 \times 10^{-5}$) and $w{=}5$ (adj.\ $p = 7.41 \times 10^{-6}$) remain significant; all other conditions are non-significant.
10	
11	\paragraph{Why it works.}
12	Three conditions jointly enable the escape: (i)~\emph{structural independence}---encoder-only DeBERTa and decoder-only Mistral share no architecture or training data (\S\ref{sec:mirage}); (ii)~\emph{positive capability gap}---verifier standalone accuracy exceeds generator SC (Finding~2); (iii)~\emph{task-verifier alignment}---NLI classification naturally matches premise-conclusion structure. Per-label complementarity: NLI excels on \textsc{False} (71.43\% vs.\ 38.10\%), SC dominates \textsc{Unknown} (68.12\% vs.\ 18.84\%). All three NLI architectures produce significant gains at $w \geq 3$ (Appendix~\ref{app:multi-nli}).
13	
14	\enlargethispage{2\baselineskip}
15	\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}
16	\looseness=-1 Four generators spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\% standalone; Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\%, below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\,pp, $p < 0.001$); LLaMA-3.1-8B ($+0.83$), Qwen2.5-14B ($+0.67$), and Qwen3-14B ($+0.50$\,pp) show no improvement. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B survives. The monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures, spanning six verifier-generator pairs (Spearman $\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; four of six share the DeBERTa verifier---see Limitations; Figure~\ref{fig:capability-gap}, Appendix~\ref{app:capability-gap}), confirming Finding~2.
17	
18	\begin{table}[t]
19	\centering
20	\small
21	\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability ($w{=}3$).}
22	\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}
23	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}
24	\toprule
25	Generator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\
26	\midrule
27	Mistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\,pp & $<$0.001 \\
28	LLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\,pp & 0.66 \\
29	Qwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\,pp & 0.50 \\
30	Qwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\,pp & 0.45 \\
31	\bottomrule
32	\end{tabular}}
33	\end{table}
34	
35	\subsection{Adaptive Verification via Disagreement Routing}
36	\label{sec:drc}
37	
38	Full SC+NLI ensemble invokes NLI on every question, but high-agreement questions gain little from external verification. \textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC)} invokes DeBERTa-large-mnli only when the fraction of $K$ traces voting for the majority answer falls below threshold $\tau$; above $\tau$, SC majority vote is used directly.
39	
40	\begin{table}[t]
41	\centering
42	\small
43	\caption{DRC on Mistral-7B ($K{=}12$, DeBERTa-large-mnli, $w{=}3$). $\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 83--90\% of full-combo gain at 35--39\% NLI cost across both datasets.}
44	\label{tab:drc}
45	\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}
46	\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}
47	\toprule
48	Dataset & Method & Acc.\ & NLI Calls & Cost & Recovery \\

50	\multirow{3}{*}{PW-D5} & SC only & 39.33\% & 0/600 & 0\% & --- \\
51	& \textbf{DRC $\tau{=}0.7$} & \textbf{45.33\%} & \textbf{233/600} & \textbf{38.8\%} & \textbf{90.0\%} \\
52	& Full combo & 46.00\% & 600/600 & 100\% & 100\% \\
53	\midrule
54	\multirow{3}{*}{FOLIO} & SC only & 55.88\% & 0/204 & 0\% & --- \\
55	& \textbf{DRC $\tau{=}0.7$} & \textbf{58.33\%} & \textbf{71/204} & \textbf{34.8\%} & \textbf{83.3\%} \\
56	& Full combo & 58.82\% & 204/204 & 100\% & 100\% \\
57	\bottomrule
58	\end{tabular}

61	\looseness=-1 DRC at $\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 90\% of full-combo gain on ProofWriter ($+6.00$ out of $+6.67$\,pp) at 38.8\% NLI cost, and 83.3\% on held-out FOLIO ($+2.45$ out of $+2.94$\,pp) at 34.8\% cost---confirming that per-question agreement routing generalizes without re-tuning $\tau$ (selected on ProofWriter; FOLIO is held-out).
```

```json
      "content": "\\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\nWe test with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), whose (premise, conclusion) structure suits NLI classification.\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\n\\looseness=-1 DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+0.49$\\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+6.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+11.00$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ (adj.\\ $p = 6.78 \\times 10^{-5}$) and $w{=}5$ (adj.\\ $p = 7.41 \\times 10^{-6}$) remain significant; all other conditions are non-significant.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\nThree conditions jointly enable the escape: (i)~\\emph{structural independence}---encoder-only DeBERTa and decoder-only Mistral share no architecture or training data (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}); (ii)~\\emph{positive capability gap}---verifier standalone accuracy exceeds generator SC (Finding~2); (iii)~\\emph{task-verifier alignment}---NLI classification naturally matches premise-conclusion structure. Per-label complementarity: NLI excels on \\textsc{False} (71.43\\% vs.\\ 38.10\\%), SC dominates \\textsc{Unknown} (68.12\\% vs.\\ 18.84\\%). All three NLI architectures produce significant gains at $w \\geq 3$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}).\n\n\\enlargethispage{2\\baselineskip}\n\\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}\n\\looseness=-1 Four generators spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\\% standalone; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\\%, below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$); LLaMA-3.1-8B ($+0.83$), Qwen2.5-14B ($+0.67$), and Qwen3-14B ($+0.50$\\,pp) show no improvement. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B survives. The monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures, spanning six verifier-generator pairs (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; four of six share the DeBERTa verifier---see Limitations; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}, Appendix~\\ref{app:capability-gap}), confirming Finding~2.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability ($w{=}3$).}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.66 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\\,pp & 0.50 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.45 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Adaptive Verification via Disagreement Routing}\n\\label{sec:drc}\n\nFull SC+NLI ensemble invokes NLI on every question, but high-agreement questions gain little from external verification. \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC)} invokes DeBERTa-large-mnli only when the fraction of $K$ traces voting for the majority answer falls below threshold $\\tau$; above $\\tau$, SC majority vote is used directly.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{DRC on Mistral-7B ($K{=}12$, DeBERTa-large-mnli, $w{=}3$). $\\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 83--90\\% of full-combo gain at 35--39\\% NLI cost across both datasets.}\n\\label{tab:drc}\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nDataset & Method & Acc.\\ & NLI Calls & Cost & Recovery \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{3}{*}{PW-D5} & SC only & 39.33\\% & 0/600 & 0\\% & --- \\\\\n& \\textbf{DRC $\\tau{=}0.7$} & \\textbf{45.33\\%} & \\textbf{233/600} & \\textbf{38.8\\%} & \\textbf{90.0\\%} \\\\\n& Full combo & 46.00\\% & 600/600 & 100\\% & 100\\% \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{3}{*}{FOLIO} & SC only & 55.88\\% & 0/204 & 0\\% & --- \\\\\n& \\textbf{DRC $\\tau{=}0.7$} & \\textbf{58.33\\%} & \\textbf{71/204} & \\textbf{34.8\\%} & \\textbf{83.3\\%} \\\\\n& Full combo & 58.82\\% & 204/204 & 100\\% & 100\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\looseness=-1 DRC at $\\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 90\\% of full-combo gain on ProofWriter ($+6.00$ out of $+6.67$\\,pp) at 38.8\\% NLI cost, and 83.3\\% on held-out FOLIO ($+2.45$ out of $+2.94$\\,pp) at 34.8\\% cost---confirming that per-question agreement routing generalizes without re-tuning $\\tau$ (selected on ProofWriter; FOLIO is held-out).\n\n",
```

```text
1	\vspace{-2mm}
2	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	\looseness=-1 We identify symbolic confirmation bias: self-extracted constraints inherit the generating model's biases ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$, 83.3\%), explaining why twenty-seven remediation strategies fail. Oracle controls ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\,pp) confirm the mechanism is sound; the bottleneck is extraction quality, not the formal channel. Three diagnostics predict failure ($C_0 < 5\%$, SC $\leq 40\%$, process similarity). Cross-architecture NLI escapes the bias when the verifier exceeds the generator ($p = 0.003$); DRC recovers 83--90\% of this gain at 35--39\% cost.
5
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01GzCZsUVsQzbD1PhxrtUJ25`</summary>

```text
1	\vspace{-1mm}
2	\section{Discussion}
3	\label{sec:discussion}
4	
5	\paragraph{Self-verification and BR magnitude.}
6	\looseness=-1 Our bias ratio ($1.66$--$4.97\times$ across nine models from 7B to frontier; Table~\ref{tab:frontier-scb}) quantifies why self-correction fails without external feedback \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification, chen2025sets}: extraction and generation share distributional biases, with nearly 5:1 on incorrect problems. Cross-model extraction gain requires both diverse traces (low $\kappa_{\text{proc}}$) and substantial self-bias (high BR); neither alone suffices (Table~\ref{tab:process-gradient}). Any self-verification pipeline deriving criteria from the same model faces this bottleneck.
7	
8	\paragraph{Test-time scaling and practitioner implications.}
9	\looseness=-1 $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$ means sampling beyond a dozen traces yields diminishing independent information; the pattern intensifies with capability (Qwen3 thinking: $\kappa = 0.91$, $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.09$), creating a scaling paradox for o1/R1-style models \citep{guo2025deepseekr1}. All non-destructive interventions cluster at $\kappa \geq 0.40$ \citep{krogh1994neural}; Debate reaches $\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\,pp). Cross-architecture escape succeeds ($p = 0.003$) because encoder-only and decoder-only models have structurally different error profiles. Two diagnostics predict failure: $C_0 < 5\%$ means SICA reduces to SC; SC $\leq 40\%$ risks error amplification ($-2.83$\,pp). For intermediate-SC models, gain tracks the capability gap monotonically ($p = 0.003$); the bottleneck is extraction quality, as oracle constraints succeed ($+29.90$\,pp, $+14.22$\,pp).
10	
```

```text
34	
35	\looseness=-1 We unify the preceding diagnostics under \textit{Cumulative Information Lift} (CIL):
36	\begin{equation}
37	\label{eq:cil-main}
38	\text{CIL}(V, G) = \log_2 \frac{P(V \text{ correct} \mid G \text{ wrong})}{P(V \text{ correct} \mid G \text{ correct})}.
39	\end{equation}
40	$\text{CIL} > 0$ when verifier $V$ preferentially corrects generator $G$'s errors; $\text{CIL} < 0$ indicates shared bias. Table~\ref{tab:cil_main} reports CIL for eight verifier--generator pairs. All cross-architecture NLI pairs on ProofWriter yield $\text{CIL} > 0$, while same-model SICA yields $\text{CIL} < 0$. CIL strongly predicts combo gain (Spearman $\rho = 0.881$, $p = 0.004$; Pearson $r = 0.854$, $p = 0.008$), unifying three findings: (1)~SCB corresponds to $\text{CIL}(\text{self}, G) \ll 0$; (2)~cross-architecture escape succeeds when $\text{CIL} > 0$; (3)~CIL decays toward zero as generator capability rises, explaining the aggregation ceiling.
41	
42	\begin{table}[t]
43	\centering
44	\small
45	\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}
46	\caption{Cumulative Information Lift across eight verifier--generator conditions. $\text{CIL} > 0$: cross-arch NLI on ProofWriter. $\text{CIL} < 0$: same-model SICA or weak capability gap.}
47	\label{tab:cil_main}
48	\begin{tabular}{@{}llrr@{}}
49	\toprule
50	Generator--Verifier & Dataset & CIL & $\Delta$ (pp) \\
51	\midrule
52	Mistral $\times$ BART-NLI & PW & $+$1.86 & $+$5.17 \\
53	Mistral $\times$ DeBERTa-NLI & PW & $+$1.67 & $+$6.67 \\
54	Mistral $\times$ RoBERTa-NLI & PW & $+$1.42 & $+$4.17 \\
55	\addlinespace
56	Qwen-14B $\times$ DeBERTa-NLI & PW & $-$0.21 & $+$0.33 \\
57	Mistral $\times$ DeBERTa-NLI & FOLIO & $-$0.47 & $+$2.94 \\
58	\addlinespace
59	Mistral $\times$ SICA & FOLIO & $-$2.93 & $+$3.43 \\
60	Qwen-14B $\times$ SICA & PW & $-$4.21 & $-$2.50 \\
61	Mistral $\times$ SICA & PW & $-$4.61 & $-$2.83 \\
62	\bottomrule
63	\end{tabular}
64	\end{table}
65	
66	\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}
67	\label{sec:diagnostics}
68	
69	Further diagnostics confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\ref{app:extraction}--\ref{app:entropy}). One positive signal emerges: Z3-based semantic deduplication removes ${\sim}$10\% additional constraints beyond syntactic dedup and yields a small but significant gain ($p = 0.013$), confirming that the formal channel adds value when redundancy is reduced. The bottleneck is not Z3 but the quality of input constraints: confirmation bias ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$) overwhelms the deduplication gain.
70	
71	
72	\subsection{Remediation Analysis}
73	\label{sec:remediation}
74	\label{sec:interventions}
75	
76	\looseness=-1 Twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning four categories---sampling diversity (temperature, multi-T, diverse prompting), extraction quality (few-shot, iterative refinement, confidence filtering), aggregation (constraint weighting, semantic dedup, MAX-SAT variants), and multi-agent (debate, self-refine, critique)---all produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Tables~\ref{tab:method_interventions}, \ref{tab:remediation}, \ref{tab:additional_interventions}). Of these, 12 represent principled strategies targeting distinct failure modes (independent constraint generation, contrastive extraction, debate-augmented reasoning, etc.), while 15 are parameter variations (temperature, prompt template, few-shot count). At the method level, Debate achieves the lowest $\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp ($p < 0.001$); all other methods cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, confirming a same-model ceiling (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}). We note an important gap: we do not evaluate trained constraint extractors (e.g., fine-tuned on oracle annotations). Our oracle controls show the extraction-quality ceiling is $+29.90$\,pp---we consider trained extractors the most promising future direction.
77	
78	\begin{figure*}[t]
79	\centering
80	\includegraphics[width=0.85\textwidth]{figures/fig_app_interventions_summary.pdf}
81	\caption{Summary of 27 intervention strategies grouped by four categories. All produce neutral-to-negative accuracy changes relative to Self-Consistency. The grey band indicates $\pm 1$\,pp noise zone.}
82	\label{fig:interventions-summary}
83	\end{figure*}
```

```json
    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\nWe test the three predictions of the confirmation bias hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Table~\\ref{tab:independence} (Appendix) reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$. Across four Mistral-7B seeds, $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$ yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp, indicating seed-level constraint quality matters beyond independence. Positive $\\Delta$ requires both sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ degrades). All four model families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\\times$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints, confirming degeneration (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}).\n\n\\looseness=-1 Bias generalizes across scale: $4.97\\times$ [3.98, 6.37] (Mistral, 12-trace), $3.93\\times$ [2.44, 7.11] (Qwen-14B, 12-trace), $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA, 4-trace$^{\\dag}$), all $> 1.0$ (95\\% bootstrap CIs for Mistral/Qwen; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}; weighted BRs in Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-bias}). SCB persists across nine models from 7B to frontier (BR $1.66$--$4.97\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}). Constraints use trace-specific variables (56.4\\% zero cross-answer overlap), collapsing aggregation to majority voting.\n\n\\looseness=-1 Two controls rule out answer leakage. First, BR is uniform across early ($5.37$), mid ($5.25$), and late ($5.16$) reasoning steps (Fisher $p = 0.71$); high-consensus errors (SC $\\geq 10/12$) amplify to $\\text{BR} = 18.8\\times$ versus $2.7\\times$ for low-consensus ($p < 0.001$). Second, removing the answer sentence from CoT traces before extraction yields no BR decrease across three truncation strategies (Wilcoxon $p \\geq 0.12$, smallest $p = 0.12$, conservative truncation; per-problem mean BR: full $2.79$, truncated $2.26$--$2.66$, computed over problems with finite ratio) despite significant accuracy loss (McNemar $p < 0.013$). SCB is consistent with a distributional prior embedded throughout reasoning, not answer contamination.\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\nWe define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:process-sim}\n\\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \\frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \\sum_{i < j} \\cos(\\mathbf{v}_i^q, \\mathbf{v}_j^q),\n\\end{equation}\n\\looseness=-1 where $\\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, mean ProcessSim $= 0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.46$: traces share reasoning processes even when answers diverge. The gap generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}), ranging from $0.31$ (LLaMA) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), revealing hidden process homogeneity that answer diversity masks. Under cross-model extraction, SICA gain decreases monotonically with ProcessSim ($\\rho = -1.0$, $p = 0.008$; Table~\\ref{tab:process-gradient}), modulated by self-bias severity.\n\n\n\\subsection{Cumulative Information Lift}\n\\label{sec:cil}\n\n\\looseness=-1 We unify the preceding diagnostics under \\textit{Cumulative Information Lift} (CIL):\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:cil-main}\n\\text{CIL}(V, G) = \\log_2 \\frac{P(V \\text{ correct} \\mid G \\text{ wrong})}{P(V \\text{ correct} \\mid G \\text{ correct})}.\n\\end{equation}\n$\\text{CIL} > 0$ when verifier $V$ preferentially corrects generator $G$'s errors; $\\text{CIL} < 0$ indicates shared bias. Table~\\ref{tab:cil_main} reports CIL for eight verifier--generator pairs. All cross-architecture NLI pairs on ProofWriter yield $\\text{CIL} > 0$, while same-model SICA yields $\\text{CIL} < 0$. CIL strongly predicts combo gain (Spearman $\\rho = 0.881$, $p = 0.004$; Pearson $r = 0.854$, $p = 0.008$), unifying three findings: (1)~SCB corresponds to $\\text{CIL}(\\text{self}, G) \\ll 0$; (2)~cross-architecture escape succeeds when $\\text{CIL} > 0$; (3)~CIL decays toward zero as generator capability rises, explaining the aggregation ceiling.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\caption{Cumulative Information Lift across eight verifier--generator conditions. $\\text{CIL} > 0$: cross-arch NLI on ProofWriter. $\\text{CIL} < 0$: same-model SICA or weak capability gap.}\n\\label{tab:cil_main}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator--Verifier & Dataset & CIL & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral $\\times$ BART-NLI & PW & $+$1.86 & $+$5.17 \\\\\nMistral $\\times$ DeBERTa-NLI & PW & $+$1.67 & $+$6.67 \\\\\nMistral $\\times$ RoBERTa-NLI & PW & $+$1.42 & $+$4.17 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen-14B $\\times$ DeBERTa-NLI & PW & $-$0.21 & $+$0.33 \\\\\nMistral $\\times$ DeBERTa-NLI & FOLIO & $-$0.47 & $+$2.94 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral $\\times$ SICA & FOLIO & $-$2.93 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nQwen-14B $\\times$ SICA & PW & $-$4.21 & $-$2.50 \\\\\nMistral $\\times$ SICA & PW & $-$4.61 & $-$2.83 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\nFurther diagnostics confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}). One positive signal emerges: Z3-based semantic deduplication removes ${\\sim}$10\\% additional constraints beyond syntactic dedup and yields a small but significant gain ($p = 0.013$), confirming that the formal channel adds value when redundancy is reduced. The bottleneck is not Z3 but the quality of input constraints: confirmation bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$) overwhelms the deduplication gain.\n\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning four categories---sampling diversity (temperature, multi-T, diverse prompting), extraction quality (few-shot, iterative refinement, confidence filtering), aggregation (constraint weighting, semantic dedup, MAX-SAT variants), and multi-agent (debate, self-refine, critique)---all produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Tables~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}, \\ref{tab:remediation}, \\ref{tab:additional_interventions}). Of these, 12 represent principled strategies targeting distinct failure modes (independent constraint generation, contrastive extraction, debate-augmented reasoning, etc.), while 15 are parameter variations (temperature, prompt template, few-shot count). At the method level, Debate achieves the lowest $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$); all other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, confirming a same-model ceiling (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}). We note an important gap: we do not evaluate trained constraint extractors (e.g., fine-tuned on oracle annotations). Our oracle controls show the extraction-quality ceiling is $+29.90$\\,pp---we consider trained extractors the most promising future direction.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\textwidth]{figures/fig_app_interventions_summary.pdf}\n\\caption{Summary of 27 intervention strategies grouped by four categories. All produce neutral-to-negative accuracy changes relative to Self-Consistency. The grey band indicates $\\pm 1$\\,pp noise zone.}\n\\label{fig:interventions-summary}\n\\end{figure*}",
```

```json
    "originalFile": "\\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\nWe test with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), whose (premise, conclusion) structure suits NLI classification.\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\n\\looseness=-1 DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+0.49$\\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+6.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+11.00$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ (adj.\\ $p = 6.78 \\times 10^{-5}$) and $w{=}5$ (adj.\\ $p = 7.41 \\times 10^{-6}$) remain significant; all other conditions are non-significant.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\nThree conditions jointly enable the escape: (i)~\\emph{structural independence}---encoder-only DeBERTa and decoder-only Mistral share no architecture or training data (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}); (ii)~\\emph{positive capability gap}---verifier standalone accuracy exceeds generator SC (Finding~2); (iii)~\\emph{task-verifier alignment}---NLI classification naturally matches premise-conclusion structure. Per-label complementarity: NLI excels on \\textsc{False} (71.43\\% vs.\\ 38.10\\%), SC dominates \\textsc{Unknown} (68.12\\% vs.\\ 18.84\\%). All three NLI architectures produce significant gains at $w \\geq 3$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}).\n\n\\enlargethispage{2\\baselineskip}\n\\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}\n\\looseness=-1 Four generators spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\\% standalone; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\\%, below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$); LLaMA-3.1-8B ($+0.83$), Qwen2.5-14B ($+0.67$), and Qwen3-14B ($+0.50$\\,pp) show no improvement. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B survives. The monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures, spanning six verifier-generator pairs (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; four of six share the DeBERTa verifier---see Limitations; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}, Appendix~\\ref{app:capability-gap}), confirming Finding~2.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability ($w{=}3$).}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.66 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\\,pp & 0.50 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.45 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Adaptive Verification via Disagreement Routing}\n\\label{sec:drc}\n\nFull SC+NLI ensemble invokes NLI on every question, but high-agreement questions gain little from external verification. \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC)} invokes DeBERTa-large-mnli only when the fraction of $K$ traces voting for the majority answer falls below threshold $\\tau$; above $\\tau$, SC majority vote is used directly.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{DRC on Mistral-7B ($K{=}12$, DeBERTa-large-mnli, $w{=}3$). $\\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 83--90\\% of full-combo gain at 35--39\\% NLI cost across both datasets.}\n\\label{tab:drc}\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nDataset & Method & Acc.\\ & NLI Calls & Cost & Recovery \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{3}{*}{PW-D5} & SC only & 39.33\\% & 0/600 & 0\\% & --- \\\\\n& \\textbf{DRC $\\tau{=}0.7$} & \\textbf{45.33\\%} & \\textbf{233/600} & \\textbf{38.8\\%} & \\textbf{90.0\\%} \\\\\n& Full combo & 46.00\\% & 600/600 & 100\\% & 100\\% \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{3}{*}{FOLIO} & SC only & 55.88\\% & 0/204 & 0\\% & --- \\\\\n& \\textbf{DRC $\\tau{=}0.7$} & \\textbf{58.33\\%} & \\textbf{71/204} & \\textbf{34.8\\%} & \\textbf{83.3\\%} \\\\\n& Full combo & 58.82\\% & 204/204 & 100\\% & 100\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\looseness=-1 DRC at $\\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 90\\% of full-combo gain on ProofWriter ($+6.00$ out of $+6.67$\\,pp) at 38.8\\% NLI cost, and 83.3\\% on held-out FOLIO ($+2.45$ out of $+2.94$\\,pp) at 34.8\\% cost---confirming that per-question agreement routing generalizes without re-tuning $\\tau$ (selected on ProofWriter; FOLIO is held-out).\n\n",
```

```json
    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\nWe test the three predictions of the confirmation bias hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Table~\\ref{tab:independence} (Appendix) reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$. Across four Mistral-7B seeds, $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$ yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp, indicating seed-level constraint quality matters beyond independence. Positive $\\Delta$ requires both sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ degrades). All four model families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\\times$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints, confirming degeneration (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}).\n\n\\looseness=-1 Bias generalizes across scale: $4.97\\times$ [3.98, 6.37] (Mistral, 12-trace), $3.93\\times$ [2.44, 7.11] (Qwen-14B, 12-trace), $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA, 4-trace$^{\\dag}$), all $> 1.0$ (95\\% bootstrap CIs for Mistral/Qwen; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}; weighted BRs in Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-bias}). SCB persists across nine models from 7B to frontier (BR $1.66$--$4.97\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}). Constraints use trace-specific variables (56.4\\% zero cross-answer overlap), collapsing aggregation to majority voting.\n\n\\looseness=-1 Two controls rule out answer leakage. First, BR is uniform across early ($5.37$), mid ($5.25$), and late ($5.16$) reasoning steps (Fisher $p = 0.71$); high-consensus errors (SC $\\geq 10/12$) amplify to $\\text{BR} = 18.8\\times$ versus $2.7\\times$ for low-consensus ($p < 0.001$). Second, removing the answer sentence from CoT traces before extraction yields no BR decrease across three truncation strategies (Wilcoxon $p \\geq 0.12$, smallest $p = 0.12$, conservative truncation; per-problem mean BR: full $2.79$, truncated $2.26$--$2.66$, computed over problems with finite ratio) despite significant accuracy loss (McNemar $p < 0.013$). SCB is consistent with a distributional prior embedded throughout reasoning, not answer contamination.\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\nWe define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:process-sim}\n\\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \\frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \\sum_{i < j} \\cos(\\mathbf{v}_i^q, \\mathbf{v}_j^q),\n\\end{equation}\n\\looseness=-1 where $\\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, mean ProcessSim $= 0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.46$: traces share reasoning processes even when answers diverge. The gap generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}), ranging from $0.31$ (LLaMA) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), revealing hidden process homogeneity that answer diversity masks. Under cross-model extraction, SICA gain decreases monotonically with ProcessSim ($\\rho = -1.0$, $p = 0.008$; Table~\\ref{tab:process-gradient}), modulated by self-bias severity.\n\n\n\\subsection{Cumulative Information Lift}\n\\label{sec:cil}\n\n\\looseness=-1 We unify the preceding diagnostics under \\textit{Cumulative Information Lift} (CIL):\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:cil-main}\n\\text{CIL}(V, G) = \\log_2 \\frac{P(V \\text{ correct} \\mid G \\text{ wrong})}{P(V \\text{ correct} \\mid G \\text{ correct})}.\n\\end{equation}\n$\\text{CIL} > 0$ when verifier $V$ preferentially corrects generator $G$'s errors; $\\text{CIL} < 0$ indicates shared bias. Table~\\ref{tab:cil_main} (Appendix) reports CIL for eight verifier--generator pairs. All cross-architecture NLI pairs on ProofWriter yield $\\text{CIL} > 0$, while same-model SICA yields $\\text{CIL} < 0$. CIL strongly predicts combo gain (Spearman $\\rho = 0.881$, $p = 0.004$; Pearson $r = 0.854$, $p = 0.008$), unifying three findings: (1)~SCB corresponds to $\\text{CIL}(\\text{self}, G) \\ll 0$; (2)~cross-architecture escape succeeds when $\\text{CIL} > 0$; (3)~CIL decays toward zero as generator capability rises, explaining the aggregation ceiling.\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\nFurther diagnostics confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}). One positive signal emerges: Z3-based semantic deduplication removes ${\\sim}$10\\% additional constraints beyond syntactic dedup and yields a small but significant gain ($p = 0.013$), confirming that the formal channel adds value when redundancy is reduced. The bottleneck is not Z3 but the quality of input constraints: confirmation bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$) overwhelms the deduplication gain.\n\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning four categories---sampling diversity (temperature, multi-T, diverse prompting), extraction quality (few-shot, iterative refinement, confidence filtering), aggregation (constraint weighting, semantic dedup, MAX-SAT variants), and multi-agent (debate, self-refine, critique)---all produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Tables~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}, \\ref{tab:remediation}, \\ref{tab:additional_interventions}). Of these, 12 represent principled strategies targeting distinct failure modes (independent constraint generation, contrastive extraction, debate-augmented reasoning, etc.), while 15 are parameter variations (temperature, prompt template, few-shot count). At the method level, Debate achieves the lowest $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$); all other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, confirming a same-model ceiling (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}). We note an important gap: we do not evaluate trained constraint extractors (e.g., fine-tuned on oracle annotations). Our oracle controls show the extraction-quality ceiling is $+29.90$\\,pp---we consider trained extractors the most promising future direction.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\textwidth]{figures/fig_app_interventions_summary.pdf}\n\\caption{Summary of 27 intervention strategies grouped by four categories. All produce neutral-to-negative accuracy changes relative to Self-Consistency. The grey band indicates $\\pm 1$\\,pp noise zone.}\n\\label{fig:interventions-summary}\n\\end{figure*}",
```

```text
62	\end{table*}
63	
64	\looseness=-1 No non-degenerate condition shows a significant SICA effect after FDR control (BH, $m{=}16$). Two show nominally significant degradation: Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); adjusted $p = 0.112$ for both. The intermediate-SC region (50--55\%) contains the only positive trends, none reaching $p < 0.05$. Four-seed replication confirms seed-level noise: two of four seeds produce null or negative $\Delta$, and the aggregate ($+3.43$\,pp) does not reach significance ($p = 0.092$). TOST equivalence tests \citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\pm 2$\,pp confirm 4 equivalent, 2 genuinely different (both negative: Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter), 10 underpowered---reflecting the power limitations at $n{=}200$--$204$ (Appendix~\ref{app:tost}). The $\pm 2$\,pp margin corresponds to the lower bound of gains reported in the SC literature \citep{wang2023sc} (2--5\,pp); $\pm 1$\,pp yields 0 equivalent conditions (0/2/14), $\pm 3$\,pp yields 10 (10/0/6). This absolute margin is more conservative at high baselines ($\pm 2.4\%$ relative at 85\%) than low ones ($\pm 5.1\%$ at 39\%), working against our equivalence claim where SICA most plausibly helps (relative $\pm 5\%$ margin yields 8 equivalent; Appendix~\ref{app:relative_tost}). We trace the failure below.
65	
66	
67	\subsection{Oracle Control}
68	\label{sec:oracle}
69	
70	Replacing self-extracted constraints with gold annotations (FOLIO logical formulae, ProofWriter DSL), Oracle-SICA yields $+29.90$\,pp on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($p < 10^{-13}$), $+14.22$\,pp on Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO ($p = 2.0 \times 10^{-7}$), and $+17.17$\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 10^{-14}$). Z3 agrees with gold labels on 95.1\% (FOLIO) and 97.1\% (ProofWriter). The MAX-SAT mechanism is sound; Mistral-7B's best self-extracted $\Delta$ ($+3.43$\,pp) captures only 11.5\% of its oracle ceiling. For Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter, the gap is wider still: self-extraction degrades accuracy ($-2.50$\,pp) against an oracle ceiling of $+17.17$\,pp, indicating that stronger models do not self-extract more effectively.
```

```json
    "originalFile": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\n\\label{sec:exp-setup}\n\nWe evaluate SICA against Self-Consistency (SC) across six models, three reasoning domains, and over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances), with diagnostic analyses of confirmation bias and process-level independence (\\S\\ref{sec:analysis}).\n\n\\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B). Frontier extension (gpt-4o, gpt-4.1, gpt-5.5, o3, Gemini-2.5-Pro) evaluates SCB universality (Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}).\n\n\\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, with gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$). StrategyQA~\\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$) serves as a domain-formalizability diagnostic. GSM8K~\\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\\%$).\n\n\\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$, extraction at $T_{\\text{ext}}{=}0.3$, $\\alpha{=}0.5$, Z3 timeout 10s. McNemar exact test ($\\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\\% bootstrap CIs ($10{,}000$ resamples) for $n \\geq 200$. SICA incurs $4.5{\\times}$ SC wall-clock; extraction accounts for 76\\% of overhead.\n\n\n\\subsection{Main Results}\n\\label{sec:main-results}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all conditions sorted by SC baseline, revealing three observation-driven accuracy regimes.\n\n\\begin{table*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{SICA vs.\\ SC across all conditions (sorted by SC). No non-degenerate condition shows a significant gain after BH correction ($m{=}16$). $\\Delta$ = SICA $-$ SC (pp). Symbols: $^b$gold ICL; $\\dagger$\\,$n < 100$; \\textbf{bold}: $p < 0.05$; {*}\\,uncorrected $p < 0.05$ (BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$); {***}\\,oracle $p < 0.001$; $^{4s}$\\,4-seed confirmatory (unmarked: single-seed exploratory).}\n\\label{tab:main_results}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrl@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Domain & $n$ & SC (\\%) & SICA (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type C: error amplification (SC $\\leq$ 40\\%)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 600 & 39.33 & 36.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}$ & 0.014{*} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Intermediate SC (SC $\\sim$50--55\\%, Mistral-7B only)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 200 & 51.00 & 51.50 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s123) & 204 & 54.41 & 57.35 & $+$2.94 & 0.070 \\\\\nMistral-7B$^{4s}$ & FOLIO & 204 & 55.88 & 59.31 & $+$3.43 & 0.092 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s456) & 204 & 55.88 & 55.88 & 0.00 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s789) & 204 & 54.41 & 53.92 & $-$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type A: confirmation bias (SC $\\geq$ 60\\%)}} \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & FOLIO & 204 & 66.18 & 61.76 & $-$4.41 & 0.093 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & StrategyQA & 200 & 72.00 & 74.00 & $+$2.00 & 0.424 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO (ICL)$^b$ & 199 & 74.37 & 74.87 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 74.00 & 71.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.50}$ & 0.011{*} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.49 & 74.51 & $-$0.98 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.98 & 76.47 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 200 & 78.50 & 78.00 & $-$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 84.80 & 84.31 & $-$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 85.00 & 85.67 & $+$0.67 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B (think) & FOLIO & 204 & 86.27 & 86.76 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Degenerate ($C_0 < 5\\%$)}} \\\\\nQwQ-32B & AIME 2024$^\\dagger$ & 22 & 72.70 & 72.70 & 0.00 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & GSM8K$^\\dagger$ & 30 & 96.67 & 96.67 & 0.00 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Oracle upper bound (gold logical annotations + Z3)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 55.88 & \\textbf{85.78} & $\\mathbf{+29.90}$ & $1.0{\\times}10^{-13}${***} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.49 & \\textbf{89.71} & $\\mathbf{+14.22}$ & $2.0{\\times}10^{-7}${***} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & ProofWriter & 600 & 74.00 & \\textbf{91.17} & $\\mathbf{+17.17}$ & $9.21{\\times}10^{-15}${***} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table*}\n\n\\looseness=-1 No non-degenerate condition shows a significant SICA effect after FDR control (BH, $m{=}16$). Two show nominally significant degradation: Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); adjusted $p = 0.112$ for both. The intermediate-SC region (50--55\\%) contains the only positive trends, none reaching $p < 0.05$. Four-seed replication confirms seed-level noise: two of four seeds produce null or negative $\\Delta$, and the aggregate ($+3.43$\\,pp) does not reach significance ($p = 0.092$). TOST equivalence tests \\citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp confirm 4 equivalent, 2 genuinely different (both negative: Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter), 10 underpowered---reflecting the power limitations at $n{=}200$--$204$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:tost}). The $\\pm 2$\\,pp margin corresponds to the lower bound of gains reported in the SC literature \\citep{wang2023sc} (2--5\\,pp); $\\pm 1$\\,pp yields 0 equivalent conditions (0/2/14), $\\pm 3$\\,pp yields 10 (10/0/6). This absolute margin is more conservative at high baselines ($\\pm 2.4\\%$ relative at 85\\%) than low ones ($\\pm 5.1\\%$ at 39\\%), working against our equivalence claim where SICA most plausibly helps (relative $\\pm 5\\%$ margin yields 8 equivalent; Appendix~\\ref{app:relative_tost}). We trace the failure below.\n\n\n\\subsection{Oracle Control}\n\\label{sec:oracle}\n\nReplacing self-extracted constraints with gold annotations (FOLIO logical formulae, ProofWriter DSL), Oracle-SICA yields $+29.90$\\,pp on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($p < 10^{-13}$), $+14.22$\\,pp on Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO ($p = 2.0 \\times 10^{-7}$), and $+17.17$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 10^{-14}$). Z3 agrees with gold labels on 95.1\\% (FOLIO) and 97.1\\% (ProofWriter). The MAX-SAT mechanism is sound; Mistral-7B's best self-extracted $\\Delta$ ($+3.43$\\,pp) captures only 11.5\\% of its oracle ceiling. For Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter, the gap is wider still: self-extraction degrades accuracy ($-2.50$\\,pp) against an oracle ceiling of $+17.17$\\,pp, indicating that stronger models do not self-extract more effectively.\n\n\n",
```

```text
35	\subsection{Adaptive Verification via Disagreement Routing}
36	\label{sec:drc}
37	
38	\looseness=-1 Full SC+NLI ensemble invokes NLI on every question, but high-agreement questions gain little from external verification. \textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC)} invokes DeBERTa-large-mnli only when the fraction of $K$ traces voting for the majority answer falls below threshold $\tau$; above $\tau$, SC majority vote is used directly.
39	
40	\begin{table}[t]
41	\centering
42	\small
43	\caption{DRC on Mistral-7B ($K{=}12$, DeBERTa-large-mnli, $w{=}3$). $\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 83--90\% of full-combo gain at 35--39\% NLI cost across both datasets.}
44	\label{tab:drc}
45	\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}
46	\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}
47	\toprule
48	Dataset & Method & Acc.\ & NLI Calls & Cost & Recovery \\
49	\midrule
50	\multirow{3}{*}{PW-D5} & SC only & 39.33\% & 0/600 & 0\% & --- \\
51	& \textbf{DRC $\tau{=}0.7$} & \textbf{45.33\%} & \textbf{233/600} & \textbf{38.8\%} & \textbf{90.0\%} \\
52	& Full combo & 46.00\% & 600/600 & 100\% & 100\% \\
53	\midrule
54	\multirow{3}{*}{FOLIO} & SC only & 55.88\% & 0/204 & 0\% & --- \\
55	& \textbf{DRC $\tau{=}0.7$} & \textbf{58.33\%} & \textbf{71/204} & \textbf{34.8\%} & \textbf{83.3\%} \\
56	& Full combo & 58.82\% & 204/204 & 100\% & 100\% \\
57	\bottomrule
58	\end{tabular}
59	\end{table}
60	
61	\looseness=-1 DRC at $\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 90\% of full-combo gain on ProofWriter ($+6.00$ out of $+6.67$\,pp) at 38.8\% NLI cost, and 83.3\% on held-out FOLIO ($+2.45$ out of $+2.94$\,pp) at 34.8\% cost---confirming that per-question agreement routing generalizes without re-tuning $\tau$ (selected on ProofWriter; FOLIO is held-out).
62	
63
```
