```json
    "oldString": "\\looseness=-1 Full SC+NLI ensemble invokes NLI on every question, but high-agreement questions gain little from external verification. \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC)} invokes DeBERTa-large-mnli only when the fraction of $K$ traces voting for the majority answer falls below threshold $\\tau$; above $\\tau$, SC majority vote is used directly.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{DRC on Mistral-7B ($K{=}12$, DeBERTa-large-mnli, $w{=}3$). $\\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 83--90\\% of full-combo gain at 35--39\\% NLI cost across both datasets.}\n\\label{tab:drc}\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nDataset & Method & Acc.\\ & NLI Calls & Cost & Recovery \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{3}{*}{PW-D5} & SC only & 39.33\\% & 0/600 & 0\\% & --- \\\\\n& \\textbf{DRC $\\tau{=}0.7$} & \\textbf{45.33\\%} & \\textbf{233/600} & \\textbf{38.8\\%} & \\textbf{90.0\\%} \\\\\n& Full combo & 46.00\\% & 600/600 & 100\\% & 100\\% \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{3}{*}{FOLIO} & SC only & 55.88\\% & 0/204 & 0\\% & --- \\\\\n& \\textbf{DRC $\\tau{=}0.7$} & \\textbf{58.33\\%} & \\textbf{71/204} & \\textbf{34.8\\%} & \\textbf{83.3\\%} \\\\\n& Full combo & 58.82\\% & 204/204 & 100\\% & 100\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\looseness=-1 DRC at $\\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 90\\% of full-combo gain on ProofWriter ($+6.00$ out of $+6.67$\\,pp) at 38.8\\% NLI cost, and 83.3\\% on held-out FOLIO ($+2.45$ out of $+2.94$\\,pp) at 34.8\\% cost---confirming that per-question agreement routing generalizes without re-tuning $\\tau$ (selected on ProofWriter; FOLIO is held-out).",
    "newString": "\\looseness=-1 Full SC+NLI ensemble invokes NLI on every question, but high-agreement questions gain little from external verification. \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC)} invokes DeBERTa-large-mnli only when the fraction of $K$ traces voting for the majority answer falls below threshold $\\tau$; above $\\tau$, SC majority vote is used directly. DRC at $\\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 90\\% of full-combo gain on ProofWriter ($+6.00$ out of $+6.67$\\,pp) at 38.8\\% NLI cost, and 83.3\\% on held-out FOLIO ($+2.45$ out of $+2.94$\\,pp) at 34.8\\% cost (Table~\\ref{tab:drc}, Appendix)---confirming that per-question agreement routing generalizes without re-tuning $\\tau$ (selected on ProofWriter; FOLIO is held-out).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\n\\looseness=-1 We test with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), whose (premise, conclusion) structure suits NLI classification.\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\n\\looseness=-1 DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+0.49$\\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+6.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+11.00$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ (adj.\\ $p = 6.78 \\times 10^{-5}$) and $w{=}5$ (adj.\\ $p = 7.41 \\times 10^{-6}$) remain significant; all other conditions are non-significant.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\n\\looseness=-1 Three conditions jointly enable the escape: (i)~\\emph{structural independence}---encoder-only DeBERTa and decoder-only Mistral share no architecture or training data (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}); (ii)~\\emph{positive capability gap}---verifier standalone accuracy exceeds generator SC (Finding~2); (iii)~\\emph{task-verifier alignment}---NLI classification naturally matches premise-conclusion structure. Per-label complementarity: NLI excels on \\textsc{False} (71.43\\% vs.\\ 38.10\\%), SC dominates \\textsc{Unknown} (68.12\\% vs.\\ 18.84\\%). All three NLI architectures produce significant gains at $w \\geq 3$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}).\n\n\\enlargethispage{2\\baselineskip}\n\\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}\n\\looseness=-1 Four generators spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\\% standalone; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\\%, below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$); LLaMA-3.1-8B ($+0.83$), Qwen2.5-14B ($+0.67$), and Qwen3-14B ($+0.50$\\,pp) show no improvement. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B survives. The monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures, spanning six verifier-generator pairs (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; four of six share the DeBERTa verifier---see Limitations; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}, Appendix~\\ref{app:capability-gap}), confirming Finding~2.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability ($w{=}3$).}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.66 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\\,pp & 0.50 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.45 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Adaptive Verification via Disagreement Routing}\n\\label{sec:drc}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Full SC+NLI ensemble invokes NLI on every question, but high-agreement questions gain little from external verification. \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC)} invokes DeBERTa-large-mnli only when the fraction of $K$ traces voting for the majority answer falls below threshold $\\tau$; above $\\tau$, SC majority vote is used directly.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{DRC on Mistral-7B ($K{=}12$, DeBERTa-large-mnli, $w{=}3$). $\\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 83--90\\% of full-combo gain at 35--39\\% NLI cost across both datasets.}\n\\label{tab:drc}\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nDataset & Method & Acc.\\ & NLI Calls & Cost & Recovery \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{3}{*}{PW-D5} & SC only & 39.33\\% & 0/600 & 0\\% & --- \\\\\n& \\textbf{DRC $\\tau{=}0.7$} & \\textbf{45.33\\%} & \\textbf{233/600} & \\textbf{38.8\\%} & \\textbf{90.0\\%} \\\\\n& Full combo & 46.00\\% & 600/600 & 100\\% & 100\\% \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{3}{*}{FOLIO} & SC only & 55.88\\% & 0/204 & 0\\% & --- \\\\\n& \\textbf{DRC $\\tau{=}0.7$} & \\textbf{58.33\\%} & \\textbf{71/204} & \\textbf{34.8\\%} & \\textbf{83.3\\%} \\\\\n& Full combo & 58.82\\% & 204/204 & 100\\% & 100\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\looseness=-1 DRC at $\\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 90\\% of full-combo gain on ProofWriter ($+6.00$ out of $+6.67$\\,pp) at 38.8\\% NLI cost, and 83.3\\% on held-out FOLIO ($+2.45$ out of $+2.94$\\,pp) at 34.8\\% cost---confirming that per-question agreement routing generalizes without re-tuning $\\tau$ (selected on ProofWriter; FOLIO is held-out).\n\n",
```

[服务器重启后首条消息] 当前日期: 2026-05-22。服务器刚重启，项目状态可能已更新。不要依赖对话历史中的状态信息，以 registry 当前值为准。

**用户消息：** [Director 指令] [Patch — Phase 1: 不依赖实验的写作修改] EMNLP 2026 ARR, DDL May 25. 8页主文严格限制。

**背景**：V16 审稿 Overall 3.0 (Findings)，需修到 3.5+ (Borderline Conference)。本轮先做所有不依赖新实验数据的修改。FOLIO 2×2 和 multi-seed 数据到位后会再派 Phase 2 patch。

## 必须修改项（按 Must-Fix 编号）

### W1: 标题 overclaiming
- 当前标题含 "from 7B to Frontier Scale" 但 frontier 模型用 cross-model extraction 不是 self-extraction
- **改标题**：去掉 "to Frontier Scale"，或改为 "from 7B to 14B, with Cross-Model Evidence at Frontier Scale"
- Abstract/Intro 必须明确区分 self-extraction 和 cross-model extraction

### W2: SCB novelty reframe
- 贡献重心从"新现象"移到"量化诊断体系 + 27策略系统排除"
- 突出 formal channel 下 SCB 的 non-obvious 之处：99% 约束通过 Z3 语法检查却仍无用
- exp-013 结果已有：MaxSAT ≈ count-only，formal verification vacuous。这比 Huang 2024 更 specific——证明即使有 provably sound verification channel，bottleneck 在 extraction 使整个 formal layer 退化为投票
- Contribution 1 降低 novelty claim 调门

### W3: 统计语言软化
- 标题中 "Do Not Improve" 改审慎措辞（如 "Show No Evidence of Improvement"）
- 已有 aggregate sign test 结果：self-extraction 5+/10- (p=0.30), all conditions 8+/15- (p=0.21)。在 Results 或 Analysis 中加一段报告 sign test
- 正文中引用 power <21% 时加 underpowered caveat
- Abstract 中 "we find no evidence of improvement" 保留但加 "across conditions that remain underpowered for small effects"

### W6: DRC 降级
- DRC 从 Contribution 4 降级为 §6 的 practical observation
- 贡献列表只保留 3 个 Contribution
- 已有 DRC τ sensitivity 数据：仅弱模型有效（Mistral +3.43pp@τ=0.7），强模型无空间。整合进 §6

### S2: CIL 重命名
- "Cumulative Information Lift" 改为 "Conditional Correction Ratio" 或类似（实际是 log-odds ratio，非 information-theoretic quantity）
- 删除 "unify" 措辞
- 已有 CIL bootstrap 结果：ρ=0.839, CI=[0.338, 0.990], LOO robust (min p=0.014)。整合进论文，acknowledge shared components (effective n < 10)

### S3: Contribution 2 reframe
- "Independence-Accuracy Trade-off" 重新措辞为"量化诊断工具集"
- 引用 Krogh & Vedelsby (1994) 明确不是新发现
- 降低 "trade-off" 的发现性措辞

### S4+S5: Display layout
- **Figure 2**（27 strategies summary）从 appendix page 25 移入 §5.6 附近
- **Table 15**（frontier SCB, 9 models）从 appendix 移入正文
- 合并 Discussion + Conclusion 省空间
- 目标：主文 display items 从 3 个增到 5-6 个

### S6: Scope 限定
- Abstract 明确 27 strategies scope 限于 training-free interventions

### S7: K_eff 用法一致性
- 统一限定 K_eff 为 ordinal diagnostic
- 删除或修改 "traces beyond a dozen add negligible independent information" 等逾越性措辞

## SCB §4.2 关键 reframe（三证据链）

**这是最重要的叙事修改**。BR shuffle control 证明：
- ❌ 旧叙事："BR 4.97× demonstrates extraction biased toward model's answer"
- ✅ 新叙事："BR_observed ≈ BR_null → constraints are informationally redundant with trace votes"

三证据链支撑 "symbolic layer is vacuous"：
1. BR shuffle: BR 反映 trace count structure，不是额外 extraction bias
2. exp-013: MaxSAT ≈ count-only，SAT solver adds nothing
3. K_eff: effective sample size 远低于 nominal

"Symbolic Confirmation Bias" 术语可保留，但重新定义为 structural（majority amplification）而非 process-level（extraction bias）。

## 已有 CPU 分析数据（直接整合进论文）

1. **BR Shuffle Control**: BR_obs ≈ BR_null (p>0.7)。加入 §5 SCB 分析
2. **Aggregate Sign Test**: 5+/10- (p=0.30) self-extraction, 8+/15- (p=0.21) all。加入 Results
3. **CIL Bootstrap**: ρ=0.839, CI=[0.338, 0.990]。替换原有单点估计
4. **DRC τ Sensitivity**: τ ∈ {0.5-0.9} sweep, only Mistral effective。加入 §6

## 不要做的
- 不要加 FOLIO 2×2 数据（Phase 2 再做）
- 不要加 multi-seed 表格（Phase 2 再做）
- 不要改 extractor confound 相关段落（等 FOLIO 2×2 结果）
- 引用暂不验证（这次不用验证引用）

## 完成后
- 编译检查（8 页限制）
- 汇报修改摘要和当前页数

### Tool result

_source line 19854 · 2026-05-22T04:21:28.848Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01G6iyhjyve6sMxFfj2MNQ6A`</summary>

```text
1	Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces saturates as sample count grows because same-model traces are correlated voters.
2	We investigate whether symbolic constraint extraction can break this plateau through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT.
3	Across six open-source LLMs (7B--32B, from Mistral-7B at 54\% SC to Qwen3-14B thinking mode at 86\% SC) and five frontier models (gpt-4o through Gemini-2.5-Pro), on three logical reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find no evidence of improvement, with equivalence established in four conditions, significant degradation in two (both in the lowest-accuracy regime), and ten conditions remaining underpowered (none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant positive effect after FDR control; Benjamini--Hochberg, $m{=}16$).
4	We identify \emph{symbolic confirmation bias} as the root cause: on incorrect problems, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC.
5	Process-level analysis reveals a deeper bottleneck: chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level Fleiss' $\kappa$ ($0.46$), indicating that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on answers.
6	Twenty-seven same-model remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail, revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off from 7B to frontier scale: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy (Debate: $\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\,pp), while methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor ($\kappa \geq 0.40$ across three model families).
7	Oracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\,pp and $+14.22$\,pp on FOLIO, $+17.17$\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of the oracle ceiling.
8	Cross-architecture NLI verification yields a significant gain when the verifier exceeds the generator (Mistral-7B $+6.67$\,pp, $p < 0.001$, $w{=}3$), with combo gain monotonically ordered by the capability gap across six verifier-generator conditions ($p = 0.003$).
9	We further propose Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC), which adaptively invokes cross-architecture verification only on low-agreement questions, recovering 83--90\% of full-combo gain at 35--39\% of verification cost across two held-out datasets.
10	
```

```text
1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	Test-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.
4	The dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.
5	SC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.
6	\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.
7	The gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.
8	
9	Confidence-weighted \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.
10	Recent neuro-symbolic methods \citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.
11	A simpler question precedes these: can the \emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?
12	No prior work has systematically evaluated this.
13	
14	We ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \citep{austensmith1996}.
15	When independence fails \citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.
16	Measuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\kappa$ \citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding $K_{\text{eff}}$ that reveals near-deterministic voting dynamics ($K_{\text{eff}} \approx 1.2$--$3.6$ for nominal $K = 12$; should be interpreted as an ordinal diagnostic rather than a literal effective sample size, \S\ref{sec:condorcet}): traces beyond a dozen add negligible independent information (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}).
17	The bottleneck runs deeper than answer-level agreement suggests.
18	Chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level $\kappa$ ($0.46$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\S\ref{sec:process-kappa}).
19	When the same model extracts logical constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: on problems where the model errs, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).
20	Self-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.
21	
22	We study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
23	Across six open-source models (7B--32B) plus five frontier models, three domains (FOLIO \citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations with four-seed replication, twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all produce neutral or negative results (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}).
24	Prior work shows LLMs struggle with natural-language self-correction \citep{huang2024selfcorrect} and that LLM-generated logical formulas can be unreliable \citep{stechly2025selfverification}. Symbolic consensus introduces an additional assumption: that formalizing reasoning into first-order logic checked by a provably sound solver (Z3) provides \emph{independent} verification evidence. Unlike natural-language self-correction, where generation and evaluation share the same neural channel, our formal channel is provably sound---the failure is precisely localizable to the extraction step. The assumption fails not because Z3 is unreliable (99\% of constraints are syntactically valid; only 3.4\% are rejected, \S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}), but because extraction inherits the model's biases, creating \emph{formalized echo chambers}.
25	
26	Oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\,pp Mistral-7B, $+14.22$\,pp Qwen-14B on FOLIO, $p < 0.001$; \S\ref{sec:oracle}), yet self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of this ceiling.
27	The central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\kappa$ to 0.15 but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\,pp); other methods cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, a same-model ceiling across three families (\S\ref{sec:remediation}).
28	A cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the confirmation bias: DeBERTa NLI yields $+6.67$\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
29	
30	Our contributions are:
31	\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]
32	\item \textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC---even under formal Z3-checked verification (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).
33	
34	\item \textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Rather than discovering a new diversity--accuracy tradeoff \citep{krogh1994neural}, we quantify its specific form in LLM self-extraction via three diagnostics ($K_\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$). Same-model $\kappa \geq 0.40$ across four methods and three families; twenty-seven strategies fail. Cross-architecture NLI yields $+6.67$\,pp ($p < 0.001$; $w{=}3$), with a consistent monotonic capability-gap trend ($p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; \S\ref{sec:remediation}, \S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
35	
36	\item \textbf{Process-Level Independence.} CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) exceeds answer-level $\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$) across four models from three families; under cross-model extraction on frontier models, SICA gain decreases monotonically with process similarity ($\rho = -1.0$, $p = 0.008$; \S\ref{sec:process-kappa}).
37	
38	\item \textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC).} A Pareto-efficient adaptive routing method that selectively invokes cross-architecture verification based on per-question trace agreement, recovering 83--90\% of full-combo gain at 35--39\% cost across two datasets (\S\ref{sec:drc}).
39	\end{enumerate}
40	
41	\begin{figure}[t]
42	\centering
43	\small
44	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{\begin{tikzpicture}[
45	    node distance=0.3cm and 0.1cm,
46	    box/.style={draw, rounded corners=2pt, minimum height=0.55cm,
47	                text width=1.2cm, align=center, font=\scriptsize},
48	    arr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick},
49	    darr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick, densely dashed},
50	    lbl/.style={font=\scriptsize, text=gray!70!black},
51	    ibottle/.style={draw=red!70!black, densely dashed, rounded corners=3pt,
52	                    inner sep=3pt},
53	]

55	\node[box, right=of q] (sample) {Sample $K$\\traces};
56	\node[box, right=of sample] (extract) {Extract logical\\constraints};
57	\node[box, right=of extract] (dedup) {Dedup.\ +\\weight};
58	\node[box, right=of dedup] (maxsat) {MAX-SAT\\(Z3)};
59	
60	\node[box, below=0.7cm of maxsat, fill=gray!12] (ans) {Answer $a^*$};
61	\node[box, below=0.7cm of sample, fill=gray!12] (sc) {Majority\\vote};
62	
63	\draw[arr] (q) -- (sample);
64	\draw[arr] (sample) -- (extract);
65	\draw[arr] (extract) -- (dedup);
66	\draw[arr] (dedup) -- (maxsat);
67	\draw[arr] (maxsat) -- (ans);
68	\draw[arr] (sample) -- node[lbl, right, yshift=0pt] {SC} (sc);
69	
70	\node[lbl, above=0.01cm of maxsat] {SICA};
71

73	
74	\draw[darr, gray!60] ([xshift=-0.1cm]dedup.south) -- ++(0,-0.35) node[lbl, below, yshift=-0.05cm] {oracle: gold FOL};
75	\end{tikzpicture}}
76	\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts logical constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}
77	\label{fig:pipeline}
```
